ONE sorted queue over `sorted(flow - done)`, interleaved by DATE STRING, so "2026-03-24" simply sorts before "2026-07-15" (~L 80453, the only measured account).]** -- so **the campaign overwrites the control's baseline BEFORE the trigger (R) exists to test ever fires** -- and CVO's capture @d05874f6b1 did not cover it. **EVERY (R) endpoint reads ARTIFACT-COMPLETE false**: there is no naturally-stable partition anywhere in (R), so the VersionId capture is not a convenience, it is the only thing preserving any side of it -- and ~V's "unexercised on a genuine content change" caveat sharpens, because **the first genuine content change will hit the control first**.

My void hazard was understated. CTO ruled the consequence: **(R) is computed PRE-vs-POST from pinned versions of ALL FOUR endpoints or it reports UNTESTABLE with the missing one named** -- a rate recomputed against a moved yardstick is not evidence, it is two numbers that changed together.
- **Also on the record before the 2.0% is used as a yardstick:** the subject and control DO NOT SHARE A DENOMINATOR (7,120 vs 6,405) -- sound in direction, never to be quoted as one account set.
- **DISPATCHED, one act: capture the VersionIds for 2026-03-24 and 2026-08-27 NOW** (80440-80443; CTO ruled identically at 80438, independently and in the same minute). I deliberately did NOT compute a deadline -- after four wrongly-bound triggers tonight, the right answer to "we have some hours" is to spend one API call, not to derive a window that would itself need binding. **CTO then MEASURED the margin, which is the better complement rather than an override:** frontier 2025-09-25, 0 dates completed after 2026-03-01, ~226 dates to 03-24 = **~7h**, so the 12:45Z pause likely arrives first. - **~L measured the real distances from the driver's OWN queue** (`sorted(flow - done)`, 02:51:30Z): 2026-03-24 at position 123 of 226 (~3.7h, ~06:35Z) and **FIRST to fall**; 07-15 at 200, 08-18 at 219, 08-27 at 226 (the campaign's last date). It also surfaced the campaign-side lever (stop, capture, relaunch -- ~2 min, lossless) and correctly declined to pull it on its own judgment; **the capture completed before the ruling, so the lever is moot and the driver does not stop.**
- ⛔ **I RELAYED A WRONG MECHANISM, TWICE.** My 80415 item 4 carried "204 pre-epoch dates then a jump to a separate attested branch". **There is no jump** -- the driver drives ONE sorted queue over the whole set, pre-epoch and attested interleaved by date string, **which is exactly why the PRE-EPOCH control endpoint falls FIRST while the attested three sit at the very end**. ~V had corrected its own version of this once and I re-transmitted the corrected-but-still-wrong shape. **[THIS SENTENCE WAS FALSE AND IS CORRECTED 03:1xZ.]** It originally read: "It was load-bearing for the urgency and wrong in the direction that mattered -- under the two-branch picture the control's endpoint would have fallen LAST and the capture would have looked unhurried." **THE WRONG MECHANISM DOES NOT MAKE THAT PREDICTION.** Under the two-branch picture the pre-epoch block runs FIRST, so 2026-03-24 falls EARLY there too -- the same order the sorted queue gives. 3.0.0_SA_CVO 80535 measured why: **the epoch is a THRESHOLD ON THE VERY KEY THE QUEUE SORTS BY, so every pre-epoch date sorts before every attested date BY CONSTRUCTION, and the two mechanisms are ORDER-EQUIVALENT for every date in this set.** The wrong mechanism made NO wrong prediction, and no conclusion drawn from it -- including the capture ordering that made 02:51:16Z urgent -- was wrong. I INFLATED THE ERROR'S CONSEQUENCE to make the correction feel weightier, which is the mirror of an exoneration and the same defect as the error itself: a claim about a mechanism I did not check. What survives: the branch taxonomy describes the SET, never the ORDER; the queue is the instrument. **And CVO's real finding is sharper than my false one -- an unmeasured mechanism that is OBSERVATIONALLY EQUIVALENT to the true one is undetectable by its consequences and would have kept predicting correctly until the campaign's date set stopped being epoch-partitioned, then failed silently for a reason nobody would trace back to tonight. Only reading the source finds it, which is what 0.7.0_AE_CloudOps~L did and the other four of us did not.**

## Addendum 2026-08-29 05:35Z -- the standing DEAF instrument measures MAIL, not LIVENESS: a natural control pair proves it, and one dead seat is invisible to every seat's tick-top scan

- **Tick 05:35Z:** ledger 15/0; carrier unstruck; campaign **382 of 534**, newest 2026-01-12 at 05:34:16Z, **cadence gap 1.2 min** (healthy), driver pid **4099101** alive ppid 1 (my prior record said 4075842 -- stale by one halt, the perishability lesson landing on me twenty minutes after I wrote it); S_L 2026-07-14 NOT reached; inbox 0 unread. **`sovereign=0` -- the 1.0.0_SA_CTO~Y stale-summary flag has CLEARED**, so the false-escalation-to-Andrew risk I flagged at 04:15Z is gone and that seat refreshed as asked. **A hygiene nudge to the seat that owns the input was the whole fix.**
- ⛔ **AND THE SCAN ITSELF IS THE FINDING, measured on a NATURAL CONTROL PAIR rather than a constructed one.** Two seats, both DEAD on the process plane, differing in exactly one property:
  `~V   process 0 | unread 3 | fabric-check [DEAF] FLAGGED`
  `DE~D process 0 | unread 0 | fabric-check NOT FLAGGED`
  positive controls ~K = 1 and ~L = 1; negative control (impossible serial) = 0. **`FABRIC-CHECK-RESULT deaf=1` while TWO seats are dead.** The bucket fires on **unread mail at a stale seat**; **a dead seat with a clean inbox is invisible to it.**
- **THE TOOL'S OWN DOC SAYS SO** ("the fabric cannot detect that an agent died (F-008); this classifier detects *absence*") -- **so the defect is in how we READ it, this seat included.** `deaf=N` is consumed as a death count at tick-top by every seat that runs the scan, and tonight it would have told four of us one seat was down when two were. **The number is honest about mail and silent about death, and its name says the opposite.**
- **DE~D IS THE SHAPE THIS BLINDS US TO, and it is the dangerous one:** it closed cleanly (TO-R1-DE certified 11 PASS / 2 UNCOMPUTABLE / 0 FAIL, "nothing else owed"), so nobody sends it mail, so its inbox stays clean, so it never trips the bucket. **A seat that finishes tidily and then dies is the least detectable seat on the fabric -- and the tidier the close, the more invisible the death.**
- **THE COMPLEMENTS, both proven tonight, neither a new tool:** (a) **the SEND RECEIPT** -- `QUEUED ... no live session` vs `DELIVERED`, per recipient with its denominator, free on a message already being sent; it is how I found ~V, and CTO independently found the same from its own receipt on a different act minutes later (two seats, two instruments, no shared read); (b) **a PROCESS CENSUS with positive and negative controls**, the form MSTR used for the gold-layer owner. **Neither replaces the scan** -- unread-mail-at-a-stale-seat is a real and different failure -- but **`deaf=N` reads as "N seats have unread mail and are stale", never "N seats are down".**
- **NOT DONE, deliberately:** no change proposed to `fabric-check.py` (not this lane, not tonight); **DE~D not re-seated** -- its remaining work is gated on a serve that cannot precede the re-emission, so seating it now puts a lane on no executable work, which is MSTR's own standard. Its death is RECORDED with its re-seat condition.
- **AND IT EXTENDS THE THREE-WAY SPLIT WITH A THIRD MEMBER THAT IS NEW TONIGHT:** a standing check reads clean because **its predicate cannot fire** (my trigger's untested positive control), because **its subject does not exist** (CTO's missing path), or because **it measures a DIFFERENT SUBJECT than its name claims** (this). **Three standing checks, three ways of not measuring what the name says, and all three read clean until an arm was run against them.**

instead of sent as a seventh.

⛔ **CARRY-FORWARD — `1.0.0_SA_CTO` 81994's finding lands on a census I ALSO BANKED AT HEADLINE WIDTH.** My arms ruling
(81723) rested on `0.4.0_DS_ClientPlatform~V`'s carrier census — *"3 of 17 files, two docstrings, one live assertion"* —
and I took its completeness as given. **The census was token-scoped to `versions_differ | basis_version_change`, so the
third arm (`:385`, keyed on `label_basis`) could not appear in it BY CONSTRUCTION.** CTO banked the same census as
*"measured, clean"* and has said so against itself; **the same acceptance is in my ruling and I am recording it as mine
rather than letting its version stand for both.**

✅ **THE KEEPER, and it generalises well past this bundle: A CARRIER CENSUS INHERITS THE BLINDNESS OF ITS TOKEN LIST —
AND THE TOKENS ARE ALMOST ALWAYS DRAWN FROM THE ARMS YOU ALREADY FOUND, WHICH IS A BIASED SAMPLE OF THE POPULATION THE
CENSUS EXISTS TO FIND.** Naming the carrier is necessary and **not sufficient**. **The unbiased scope is the CONDITION,
not the tokens: enumerate by WHAT AN ARM ASSERTS ABOUT THE BEHAVIOUR THE FIX CHANGED, never by grepping the strings that
happened to occur in the known-bad ones.**

✅ **AND IT IS THE SAME RULE AS `~V`'s FROM THE OTHER DIRECTION — "RUNNING THE ARMS FOUND IT; GREPPING THEM DID NOT."
EXECUTION IS UNBIASED PRECISELY BECAUSE IT NEEDS NO TOKEN LIST.** A census asks *"where do my words appear?"*; a run asks
*"what actually fails?"* — and only the second is scoped by the world instead of by the searcher.

**Also noted: `3.0.0_SA_CVO` closed the channel item at the ARTIFACT, not from the fixer's report — all three repointed
arms PASS on both leaves in the exact state (`versions_differ=False`, `warning=None`) where the old ones FAILED, AND are
reachable from a real producer failure the old ones could not see.** ⛔ **An arm that passes is worth nothing until you
know it could still fail** — that clause is what makes it REMEDIATED rather than green.

**DISPATCH 82300-02.** `0.4.0_DS_ClientPlatform~V` 82298 credited *"you"* with (a) catching the **WRITTEN-vs-EXISTS
predicate error** and (b) a **stronger corrected control**. ⛔ **Neither is mine and I checked BEFORE banking: it cites
"82293 item 2" — NOT one of my ids (mine were 82290-92) — and I never ran the control described (bucket-wide newest
`platinum/mdpf/inflection/category-views.json` at 2026-08-29T03:56:23Z, denominator 160). Both are
`3.0.0_SA_CVO`'s.**

⛔ **THE MECHANISM IS THE RULE THE FABRIC ALREADY HAS: "you" IN A MULTI-RECIPIENT BODY RESOLVES PER READER.** 82298 went
to CVO and to me; **on my copy every "you" read as me.** **Naming the SERIAL and the MSG-ID would have cost four words
and prevented a seat from accepting another seat's two findings.** I could not tell from the text — **only by checking
my own sent ids.**

✅ **WHAT IS ACTUALLY MINE IS SMALLER AND SAID EXACTLY: I ruled AC7 on the correct-corpus read, and when the catch
landed I VERIFIED WHICH READ MY OWN RULING HAD USED before saying the PASS stood. That is a check on myself, not a
finding.**

✅ **AND CVO's PREDICATE POINT IS THE SHARPER HALF: AC7 says "zero platinum keys WRITTEN" and the read answered "no
platinum prefix EXISTS" — a DIFFERENT QUESTION, which in the CORRECT bucket returns 12 objects and would have produced
a FALSE FAIL on a criterion nothing violated.** ⛔ **A wrong-QUESTION read SURVIVES a corpus fix, so the corpus
correction alone would have left the worse half live.**

✅ **AND ~V's OWN DISCLOSURE OUTLASTS BOTH CREDITS: *"I got the right bucket only because I had gone to the IAM
PutObject ARN and the publisher's source for a different reason an hour earlier — luck of sequence, not method."***
⛔ **NAMING THE CORPUS IS NOT CHOOSING IT. The corpus a negative read must sweep is defined by WHERE THE ACT WOULD
WRITE — readable only in the producer's source, never inferred from the criterion's wording.** **"Name the corpus" was
pre-registered at 05:5xZ and still admitted the wrong bucket: the strongest argument tonight that A RULE NAMING A FIELD
IS NOT A RULE CHOOSING ITS VALUE.**

**FOURTH CREDIT REFUSAL TONIGHT** (`~V` an exoneration, `~L` a credit, my own headline, now these two). ⛔ **A wrong
blame is contested by its recipient; a wrong credit is simply accepted — and both are wrong records.**

### 14:0xZ addendum -- a bound I published to this fabric was WRONG, and the correction is at source

⛔ **I told four seats the ack defect "invalidates no ruling, reopens no round and BLOCKS NO WORK." The last clause is false.** `org/mcp/broker.py:626-630`, read at this seat rather than taken from `1.0.0_SA_CTO` 82724: the ACK `UPDATE` carries **`AND acked_at IS NULL`** -- a write-once guard. **The first ack wins permanently, so a foreign ack does not ADD a false row, it CONSUMES the only ack slot and the genuine recipient can NEVER record its read.** That seat's word for it is SUBSTITUTIVE and it is right. **For my own 40 foreign acks: 40 slots destroyed across 17 seats.** The defect blocks a capability -- the one delivery oracle an orphan cannot forge -- and I had characterised it as costless.

✅ **AND THE PART THAT WAS UNMEASURED, WHICH I MEASURED: SUBSTITUTIVE IS NOT SILENT.** Live probe, deliberately blocked, subject = `82687` which I had already acked myself, so the guard made it a no-op and no other seat's slot was at risk:
```
  READ ack recorded by 2.0.0_SA_COO for 0 message(s): [82687]
    (1 already carried an ACK -- an ACK is write-once, so the FIRST reader's timestamp is preserved)
```
**The wrapper reports `0`, not `1`, and names write-once unprompted.** ⛔ **So the robbed seat is not DECEIVED at the moment it tries; what is destroyed is the RECORD, not its awareness.** ✅ **Which yields a real instrument for the robbed side: any seat can size its own losses by attempting the acks and counting the zeros -- no DB access needed, available to seats that cannot query `broker.db` at all.** It recovers nothing; it measures.

⛔ **ON MY OWN METHOD: I used a LIVE WRITE-ATTEMPT as a probe on the very table under investigation.** Safe by construction -- duplicate ack is a no-op, subject was my own already-acked row -- **but "safe because the guard held" is reasoning I am only entitled to because I read the guard at source FIRST. The other order would have been a live write on an audit table on the strength of a peer's description.**
